## Document 254: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-08).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-09-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Commodities: From Inflation Hedge to Structural Growth Asset
Anna Snider, Managing Director and Head of Investment Manager Selection
(Due Diligence)
A broader role in portfolios: For decades, commodities occupied a relatively narrow
role in portfolios: protection against inflation when rising prices eroded the value
of traditional assets. That function remains important, but the investment case is

broadening. Commodities now sit at the intersection of geopolitical fragmentation,
supply-chain security, reshoring, inventory rebuilding, and the buildout of AI and digital
infrastructure. Together, these forces are turning parts of the asset class from purely
cyclical exposures into potential long-term growth beneficiaries.
The diversification case is also becoming more relevant. In Q1, when inflation concerns
peaked, the Bloomberg Commodity Index returned close to 25%, outperforming both

Equities and Fixed Income. At the same time, the relationship among major asset
classes has shifted. Equity and Fixed Income correlations have tightened in recent years,
while commodities have moved in the opposite direction: over the trailing five years,
their correlation with both Equities and bonds has been negative. That combination of
inflation sensitivity and differentiated return behavior can make commodities useful
beyond a simple tactical hedge.

AI infrastructure is a physical buildout: AI is often viewed through semiconductors,
software platforms, and hyperscale data centers. Yet the infrastructure required to
deploy AI is extraordinarily commodity intensive. It requires continuous electricity,
generation capacity, transmission lines, substations, transformers, cooling systems,
networking hardware and structural materials. In that sense, AI is not only a computing
revolution. It is also an energy, infrastructure, and critical-materials investment cycle.

Power is the first constraint. Data centers require reliable, around-the-clock electricity.
Natural gas remains a practical source of incremental generation in the U.S., while
nuclear power is emerging as a strategic long-term option for hyperscalers seeking
stable baseload supply. Solar and wind can add capacity and support decarbonization
goals, but grid reliability and storage become more important as power demand rises.
That supports investment in batteries, backup power and grid-scale storage, with lithium

and graphite among the relevant inputs.
The buildout also increases demand for industrial metals. Copper is a clear beneficiary
because it is essential to transmission, transformers, cooling systems and data-center
wiring. Aluminum and iron ore are used in facilities, electrical equipment, transmission
networks and supporting infrastructure. Semiconductor fabrication adds another layer
of demand through industrial gases such as helium and neon, high-purity quartz for

wafers and optics, and gallium and germanium for advanced chips, photonics, and
telecommunications. AI-enabled automation and robotics also depend on permanent
magnets that use rare earth elements, including neodymium-praseodymium and
dysprosium-terbium.
Demand has moved faster than supply: This demand surge resembles earlier cycles
in semiconductors and memory, where expectations pulled spending forward. Metals
and mining companies are already seeing earnings growth and margin expansion,

yet the industry has struggled to keep pace with demand. The imbalance matters
because commodity supply is slow to respond. Production of many strategic materials
is geographically concentrated, permitting timelines are lengthy, recycling capacity is
limited, and substitution can remain years from commercial viability. New mines and
processing facilities can take a decade or more to reach production.
Investment Implications
Commodities may increasingly serve

three portfolio purposes: inflation
protection, diversification from
traditional assets, and participation in
long-term infrastructure and resource-
demand growth, supporting a diversified
allocation across direct commodities
and select producer Equities rather than
a single directional trade.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Governments are also taking a larger role through industrial policy, export controls,
stockpiling and efforts to secure critical supply chains. Restrictions involving gallium,
germanium, graphite and other strategic materials underscore how geopolitics can affect
availability and pricing. As a result, the current cycle may produce greater scarcity and
pricing power than demand growth alone would suggest.

So far, this dynamic has been most visible in industrial metals, but the same forces could
extend into agriculture. Reshoring changes where food and inputs are produced and
processed, while inventory rebuilding can lift demand for fertilizer, fuel, machinery and
storage. Agriculture has not yet experienced the same earnings and pricing impact as
metals, but supply-chain resilience could make it a broader beneficiary over time.
Cyclical forces still matter: The structural thesis does not eliminate near-term

volatility. Energy remains a key transmission mechanism for inflation because oil and
natural gas underpin transportation, manufacturing and industrial production. Geopolitical
disruptions can cascade through supply chains, raising costs and moving both commodity
futures and spot prices. Currency dynamics matter as well. A stronger U.S. dollar can
restrain commodity prices by increasing costs for non-U.S. buyers, while renewed dollar
weakness could support emerging-market demand and reprice dollar-denominated

assets.
A stabilization of geopolitical tensions could create a more constructive cyclical backdrop
through lower energy volatility, improved business confidence and stronger capital
investment. Conversely, renewed conflict can produce sharp and uneven moves across
the complex. These crosscurrents reinforce the value of treating commodities as a
diversified allocation rather than a single directional trade.
Gold, implementation and the portfolio case: Gold is fundamentally different from

most industrial commodities, but it deserves attention. Its traditional relationships with
interest rates and the dollar have been less reliable lately. Even so, sustained central bank
and exchange-traded fund buying points to durable long-term demand despite bouts of
volatility such as the selloff earlier this year. Gold also tended to move inversely with oil
during the height of the Iran conflict, illustrating how different commodities can respond

differently to the same geopolitical shock.
Investors can access the theme through commodity futures or producer Equities. Direct
commodity exposure is generally the clearer vehicle for inflation protection, scarcity, and
geopolitical supply shocks. Producer equities can offer greater upside when commodity
prices rise faster than operating costs because earnings and margins expand, but that
leverage is asymmetric. Company-specific execution, financing, cost and valuation risks

can still cause Equities to underperform the underlying commodity. A blended approach
may capture both physical scarcity and corporate operating leverage.
Ultimately, commodities remain sensitive to energy markets, currencies, growth, and
geopolitics, but their portfolio role is expanding. They can provide inflation protection and
diversification while offering exposure to AI infrastructure, electrification, energy security,
defense modernization, reshoring and, potentially, agricultural supply-chain investment.

The strongest beneficiaries of the AI trade may therefore extend well beyond technology
companies to the materials and power systems that make deployment possible.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Rising Global Interest Rates and International Markets
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Strong earnings growth has so far delivered a fourth consecutive year of double-digit
gains for global Equity indexes in 2026, propelling them to new all-time highs. But a
growing risk throughout the year has been the increase in government bond yields, which

have also broken out to multi-decade highs across a number of the major markets. The
U.S. Treasury will this week begin its expanded Treasury securities buyback program
announced in mid-August in response to rising rates. But 10-year yields across all
the major G10 countries have moved higher, in many cases by as much as or more
than in the U.S. market. Against a backdrop of above-trend growth in nominal gross
domestic product (GDP), high energy prices and elevated inflation, investors appear to

be reassessing monetary policy expectations, particularly in Europe where most of the
2026 increase in 10-year yields has been driven by inflation breakevens (Exhibit 1). Fixed
Income markets are consequently looking for policy rate hikes from the European Central
Bank later this week and potentially from the Bank of Japan later next month.
The rise in global interest rates has also come alongside an expansion in fiscal policy
across the major markets. In Europe, 2026 and 2027 are expected to be the peak

spending years for defense and infrastructure expenditure plans unveiled last year. The
so-called Readiness 2030 package allocates €800 billion to increased European Union
defense spending as it looks to move toward its 5% of GDP target by 2035. And at the
same time, the German government relaxation of its national debt brake has directed
€500 billion toward funding for investment in clean energy, transportation and digital
infrastructure. Similarly in Japan, the government supermajority is looking to enact a

new stimulus aimed at raising investment levels across 17 strategic sectors such as
semiconductors, defense and shipbuilding, in addition to a planned two-year cut to the
consumption tax on food from April 2027. And in the UK, the new central government
leadership is also likely to announce new spending measures when its first national
budget is released next month. To the extent that increased public investment spending
can lift productivity and support nominal GDP growth in Europe and Japan, the rise in

yields need not undermine economic growth or derail the equity market advance. But
higher rates, especially in response to supply-driven energy price inflation, nonetheless
remain a risk for developed market equity returns going into the end of the year.
Exhibit 1: Global Bond Yields in Major Markets have Risen by as Much or More
than U.S. Yields in 2026.

For many emerging markets the picture looks quite different. In contrast with the U.S.,
Japan and Europe, inflation rates are generally well below central bank targets outside
a limited number of resource exporters in Latin America and the booming AI-linked
economies of Korea and Taiwan (Exhibit 2). As a result, policy rate expectations over

the coming quarters are stable or lower for several emerging markets (including China,
Malaysia, Thailand, Poland, Brazil and Mexico) according to Bloomberg consensus forecasts.
Investment Implications

and corporate earnings should support
global equity markets even in the face
of rising rates. For Emerging Markets
(EM) Equities in particular, contained
inflation, flat to lower local policy rate
expectations and stronger fundamentals

should limit the impact. And we
continue to favor EM Equities over
International Developed Markets, where
inflation levels are generally still above
target, central banks are expected
to raise rates and investors will be
watching for fiscal sustainability as
governments implement expansionary
budget plans.

Historically, rising U.S. interest rates have been a major headwind for emerging markets.
But looking ahead in the current cycle, we still expect the impact of potential Federal
Reserve (Fed) rate hikes to be lower than in the past. Over recent years, the association
between tighter Fed policy and weaker EM returns appears to have diminished. EM
Equities for example were resilient in the face of aggressive U.S. rate increases in 2022
and 2023, broadly keeping pace with the rest of the world. This is in sharp contrast to

prior cycles in which tighter Fed policy has resulted in capital flight, deleveraging and
falling asset values across the emerging world. Particularly during the crisis periods of
the 1980s and 1990s—but also more recently during the 2015 taper tantrum—U.S.
monetary tightening has tended to attract capital away from riskier emerging countries
(particularly those with large current account deficits), reducing support for local asset
prices and exchange rates.

Associated strength in the U.S. dollar has typically been an additional hurdle. For those
emerging economies with fixed exchange rates, a loss of export competitiveness has
further undermined current account positions, forcing central banks to run down their
foreign exchange reserves. And where exchange rates are floating, foreign currency
debts have risen in local currency terms, resulting in widespread defaults, debt
downgrades, recessions and additional exchange rate depreciation.

But starting in the years that followed the 1997-1998 Asian financial crisis and
continuing into the current cycle, the fundamentals across much of the EM universe have
improved. Fixed exchange rates have been abandoned. EM foreign currency reserves have
roughly doubled as a share of GDP . The share of EM debt denominated in U.S. dollars
has fallen significantly, led by the private sector across households and corporates. And
for most countries, current account deficits have narrowed or disappeared entirely. As a

result, emerging markets have become much more insulated from the direction of U.S.
interest rates on a structural basis, and this has only been reinforced by the AI-driven
revaluation of key Asian markets in 2026. Greater concentration in Korea and Taiwan has
increased the EM weighting in the surplus Asia-Pacific region to 83% as at the end of
August—up from 81% at the start of the year and 71% a decade ago.
Above-trend growth in nominal GDP and ongoing strength in corporate earnings should

support global equity markets even in the face of rising interest rates. But for emerging
Equities in particular, contained inflation relative to central bank targets, flat to lower
local policy rate expectations and stronger economic fundamentals should limit the
impact from higher global rates. We continue to favor EM Equities over International
Developed Markets, where inflation levels are generally still above target, central banks
are expected to raise interest rates and investors will be watching for fiscal sustainability

as governments implement expansionary budget plans.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The One-Offs Inflating Q2 Earnings
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Headline Q2 earnings numbers look almost too good to be true. And in a sense, they are.
With nearly all of S&P 500 companies’ results reported, EPS growth is tracking around
50% year-over-year (YoY)—not just an acceleration from Q1 but a second consecutive
quarter above 20%. Chiefly skewing the headline number are massive mark-to-market

gains on private investments for a handful of mega cap technology companies like
Alphabet and Amazon that booked outsized non-operating gains tied to their stakes in
Anthropic, while Microsoft benefited from its OpenAI investment. Strip those out, and
adjusted EPS growth notches down to 31%. The point being, after being sliced and
diced, 30%+ is well above any long-run historical earnings growth rate.
The one-off nature of earnings strength wasn’t limited to investment gains. A second,

less-discussed boost was tariff refunds. As Exhibit 3 illustrates, revenues from tariffs
steadily climbed from early 2025 through mid-2026 until the International Emergency
Economic Powers Act (IEEPA) tariff ruling which initiated the refund process. As
refunds were disbursed, customs receipts turned net negative in June and July, with
refund payments exceeding total tariff collections. Customs and Border Protection cash
withdrawals of more than $100 billion in refunds have now been paid—60% of the $165

billion in total eligible claims.
The recipients: the “importers of record,” or the dozens of S&P 500 companies that
have disclosed billions in refunds over the past two quarters; Apple alone received $2.19
billion, adding $0.11 to per-share earnings to their quarter. Other disclosed refunds
include Nike with $986 million, Amazon with $640 million, and General Motors refunded
$500 million (although accounting treatment and use of proceeds has varied).

The boost of investment gains and/or refunds may not be repeatable in future quarters.
Perhaps more telling is that the median company’s revenue grew roughly 15% YoY, the
strongest gain in years, corroborating that there is operating momentum rather than
just an accounting mirage for Q2 earnings. Margins, too, as a percent of GDP are at
their highest back to 1950, hovering north of 22%, see Exhibit 4. That this is occurring
amid rising rates and at a time of geopolitical uncertainty/disruption, rather than a post-

recessionary recovery, makes the results that much more impressive.
The Path Forward: Questions remain about the sustainability of these elevated growth
rates, although consensus expects EPS growth to stay above 20% in both Q3 and Q4.
If achieved, every quarter this year would see 20%-plus EPS growth. On the back of
brighter outlooks from Corporate America in recent weeks, full-year earnings growth
will decelerate into the mid-teens in 2027, but that moderation comes off a high base

of 2026. For now, the breadth of earnings growth is the encouraging story as 10 of
the 11 sectors reported growth (Energy and Communication Services leading the way,
Healthcare the only detractor). That’s a reflection of strong core businesses growing
revenues into 2027’s tougher YoY comparisons.
Investment Implications
Underlying earnings-per-share (EPS)
growth of around 30%, stripped
of investment gains, is historically
exceptional. It remains that robust

corporate earnings and positive
revisions have helped Equities absorb
concerns around the recent rise
in yields.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

2Q2026, SCS ghi nhận doanh thu 279 tỷ VND (-4% yoy), LNST đạt 188 tỷ VND (đi 
ngang svck). Lũy kế 1H2026, doanh thu đạt 551 tỷ VND (-1% yoy), LNST đạt 361 tỷ 
VND (+0.5% yoy), trong đó thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh bù đắp phần lớn sự suy 
giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng mùa cao điểm hàng hóa cuối năm và mạng bay Trung Đông 
phục hồi sẽ hỗ trợ sản lượng SCS trong những tháng cuối năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ 
cải thiện còn hạn chế do nhu cầu vận tải trên các tuyến Trung Đông – châu Á và 
châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn suy yếu. Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự 
kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy).

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sau khi các hạ tầng kết nối với sân bay Long Thành được hoàn 
thiện, tỷ lệ phân bổ chuyến bay quốc tế giữa Long Thành và Tân Sơn Nhất sẽ dao 
động quanh khoảng 75%/25%. Tỷ trọng phân bổ hàng quốc tế tại TP. Hồ Chí Minh 
qua Long Thành 2027/2028/2029 dự kiến sẽ đạt 10%/35%/55%.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì cổ tức tiền mặt cao nhờ khả năng tạo dòng 
tiền ổn định và quy mô tiền gửi lớn. Cổ tức năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ 
phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027 nhằm tích lũy 
nguồn lực cho các dự án mới.

### Page 2

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ hàng hóa Sài Gòn được thành 
lập năm 2008, hiện là đơn vị khai thác nhà ga 
hàng hóa hàng không lớn thứ 2 tại cảng 
hàng không quốc tế (HKQT) Tân Sơn Nhất 
với thị phần khoảng 50%. Các cổ đông lớn 
của SCS bao gồm CTCP Gemadept (33.4%) 
và Tổng Công ty Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam 
(13.6%). 
Hoạt động khai thác nhà ga là mảng kinh 
doanh chính của SCS. Hiện SCS đang tiếp 
tục mua sắm trang thiết bị nâng công suất 
xử lý hàng lên 350,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy) dù đã được hỗ 
trợ bởi mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi và mùa cao điểm cuối năm 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Thông lượng hàng hóa quốc tế qua SCS bắt đầu giảm từ 2027 với 10%/35%/55%/70% hàng quốc tế 
Tân Sơn Nhất phân bổ sang cho L TA vào 2027/2028/2029/2030, trước khi bắt đầu tăng trưởng trở lại từ 
2031. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định nhờ nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh, cổ tức 2026 
dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu và duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
KQKD 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ

2Q2026, SCS ghi nhận doanh thu 279 tỷ VND (-4% yoy), LNST đạt 188 tỷ VND (đi 
ngang yoy). Lũy kế 1H2026, doanh thu đạt 551 tỷ VND (-1% yoy), LNST đạt 361 
tỷ VND (+0.5% yoy), trong đó thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh bù đắp phần lớn sự 
suy giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga.

### Page 4

Biểu đồ 4. Cơ cấu thông lượng hàng SCS (tấn)  Biểu đồ 5. Giá dịch vụ và biên LNG hoạt động khai thác nhà ga 
của SCS

Nguồn: SCS  Nguồn: SCS

Mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi hỗ 
trợ sản lượng SCS trong 2H2026, 
song mức độ cải thiện còn hạn chế 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng hàng hóa của SCS sẽ cải thiện trong những tháng 
cuối năm 2026 nhờ mạng bay Trung Đông dần được khôi phục, đặc biệt từ 
Qatar Airways, cùng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa tăng trong mùa cao điểm cuối 
năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ phục hồi dự kiến còn hạn chế khi nhu cầu vận tải trên 
các tuyến Trung Đông – Châu Á và Châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn giảm lần lượt 
14.1% và 16.1% yoy trong tháng 7/2026 (tháng thứ 5 liên tiếp), trái ngược với 
mức tăng 3.9% của thị trường hàng hóa hàng không toàn cầu. Diễn biến này 
phần nào giải thích việc sản lượng hàng quốc tế của SCS tiếp tục giảm khoảng 
10% yoy trong tháng 7–8, dù tần suất bay của các hãng Trung Đông đã phục 
hồi đáng kể.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng hàng quốc tế và nội địa trong bốn tháng cuối 
năm lần lượt giảm 4% và 2% yoy, cả năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy) và 
52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy). Doanh thu khai thác nhà ga được kỳ vọng tiếp tục 
được hỗ trợ bởi việc điều chỉnh tăng giá dịch vụ từ đầu năm, bù đắp một phần 
tác động từ sản lượng suy giảm.

Biểu đồ 6. Mức độ phục hồi chuyến bay của các hãng hàng không vùng Vịnh 
Đến ngày 21/09/2026, tần suất bay của các 
hãng vùng Vịnh đã phục hồi đáng kể, phần 
lớn đạt khoảng 85–100% mức trước xung đột, 
riêng Qatar Airways đạt 97%, tăng từ mức 
87% ghi nhận giữa tháng 6.

### Page 5

Long Thành tạo áp lực lên sản 
lượng và biên lợi nhuận từ 2027, 
triển vọng dài hạn phụ thuộc khả 
năng tham gia vận hành nhà ga mới 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa hàng không khu vực phía Nam duy 
trì tăng trưởng trong trung hạn, với sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa tăng 8%/4% 
yoy năm 2027 và 9%/6% mỗi năm từ 2028, được hỗ trợ bởi hoạt động xuất 
nhập khẩu, thương mại điện tử và sự phát triển của chuỗi cung ứng. Tuy nhiên, 
sản lượng hàng quốc tế qua SCS dự kiến chịu áp lực khi các chuyến bay từng 
bước chuyển sang Long Thành. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi giả định tỷ lệ 
chuyến bay quốc tế chuyển sang Long Thành lần lượt đạt 10%/35%/55%/70% 
trong giai đoạn 2027–2030, với tác động lên sản lượng thực tế phụ thuộc vào 
tiến độ khai thác nhà ga hàng hóa và hệ thống logistics tại sân bay mới.

Đồng thời, giá dịch vụ quốc tế dự kiến bắt đầu giảm từ giữa năm 2027 nhằm giữ 
chân các hãng hàng không, trong khi giá nội địa kỳ vọng duy trì tăng 3%/năm. 
Theo đó, biên lợi nhuận gộp dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong năm 2027 và chịu áp lực 
đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ trọng hàng quốc tế có mức giá dịch vụ cao suy giảm.

Bên cạnh đó, SCS có cơ hội mở rộng quy mô khai thác trong dài hạn thông qua 
việc tham gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành, công suất thiết kế 
550 nghìn tấn/năm. Doanh nghiệp đã ký biên bản ghi nhớ với ACV về khả năng 
tham gia vận hành nhà ga này, qua đó có thể tiếp cận nguồn hàng dịch chuyển 
từ Tân Sơn Nhất nếu được lựa chọn. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu 
từ dự án vào kịch bản cơ sở khi chưa có kết quả lựa chọn đơn vị vận hành chính 
thức.

Biểu đồ 7. Thông lượng hàng và biên LNG của SCS 2022 – 2028F

Nguồn: SCS, KBSV dự phóng

Nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh và 
chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định 
hỗ trợ định giá cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định trong 
những năm tới nhờ khả năng tạo dòng tiền cao, quy mô tiền gửi lớn và mức 
đòn bẩy tài chính thấp. Trong 1H2026, thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh đã bù đắp 
phần lớn sự suy giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga, giúp LNST duy 
trì ổn định ở mức 361 tỷ VND (+0.5% yoy). Chúng tôi dự phóng cổ tức tiền mặt 
năm 2026 tiếp tục đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 
VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027, phù hợp với triển vọng lợi nhuận chịu áp lực từ 
Long Thành và nhu cầu tích lũy nguồn lực tài chính để tham gia các dự án mới.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu  
 Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền (DCF), trên cơ sở cập nhật triển 
vọng kinh doanh và tác động từ quá trình chuyển đổi khai thác sang Long 
Thành, chúng tôi đưa ra giá mục tiêu cho SCS là 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương với mức tiềm năng sinh lời 14.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 
22/09/2026. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu từ việc tham 
gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành vào định giá. Các giả định 
chính bao gồm: 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc tế năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 4% yoy do mạng bay Trung Đông chưa phục 
hồi hoàn toàn. Nhu cầu hàng quốc tế khu vực phía nam dự kiến tăng 
8% năm 2027 và 9%/năm trong giai đoạn 2028–2030, kéo theo lượng 
hàng qua SCS trong 2027/2028/2029/2030 sẽ giảm lần lượt -2.8%/-
21%/-25%/-27% yoy, trước khi tăng trưởng trở lại từ 2031 sau khi đạt 
tỷ trọng phân bổ tối ưu giữa SGN và LTA. 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc nội năm 2026 đạt 52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 2% yoy do áp lực chi phí vận tải và điều 
chỉnh mạng bay. Sản lượng dự kiến phục hồi 4% năm 2027 và 6%/năm 
từ 2028 khi chi phí nhiên liệu hạ nhiệt và năng lực khai thác cải thiện. 
- Giá nội địa dự kiến tăng 3%/năm, trong khi giá quốc tế bắt đầu giảm 
từ giữa 2027 nhằm giữ chân các hãng hàng không. Biên lợi nhuận gộp 
dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong 2027 và chịu áp lực đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ 
trọng hàng quốc tế suy giảm và chi phí cố định không giảm tương ứng

## Document 276: CTCP TCT Nước - Môi trường Bình Dương - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23)

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước: 
tại đây 
BWE hưởng lợi từ khung giá xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt, giúp doanh thu đạt 1,069/1,234 tỷ VND trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Về diễn biến thị trường, BWE hiện đang giao dịch quanh mức -1 độ lệch chuẩn 
trung bình 5 năm EV/EBITDA, phản ánh những bất ổn trong thương mại toàn 
cầu ảnh hưởng tới tiêu thụ nước phục vụ sản xuất. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh 
giá triển vọng trong tương lai của mảng môi trường (bao gồm nước thải và rác 
thải) cộng với chiến lược đa dạng hóa địa bàn cung cấp nước sẽ bù đắp được 
KQKD tăng trưởng chậm lại.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA 
với BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ 
vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026. Mức EV/EBITDA mục tiêu cho 
việc định giá doanh nghiệp là 8.8 lần, tương ứng với mức trung bình 5 năm của 
doanh nghiệp, để phản ánh sự cải thiện KQKD đến từ mảng môi trường và cơ 
chế giảm giá mua nước từ TDM sẽ bù đắp sự giảm tốc của mảng cấp nước trong 
ngắn hạn. Với mức giá mục tiêu trên, cổ phiếu được định giá tương đương P/B 
1.7x cho 2026, phù hợp với việc ROE được kỳ vọng cải thiện lên mức 14.1%.

## Document 284: EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

## Document 289: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

### Page 2

2 01 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

I NSTI TUTE

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 313: Heatwaves have exposed a blind spot in Belgium’s housing stock

Nearly nine in 10 Belgians say their home was uncomfortably
hot in 2026, exposing a blind spot in the renovation debate:
energy eﬃciency alone does not guarantee summer comfort.
Belgium therefore faces a dual renovation challenge of
cutting energy use while adapting homes to a warmer climate
Alissa Lefebre
Belgium faces a dual renovation challenge of cutting energy use while
adapting homes to a warmer climate
Heatwaves affected daily life in Belgian

homes this summer
Heat aﬀected a signiﬁcant number of Belgian homes in 2026.
According to our ING Renovation survey of 1,001 Belgians, nearly
nine in 10 said their home was uncomfortably hot for several days,
while one in four said the discomfort lasted more than two weeks.
Only 10% experienced no uncomfortable heat at home.
The problem is not conﬁned to older housing stock: 82% of
residents in homes built after 2011 reported uncomfortable indoor

Energy efﬁciency does not tell the full story
Seventy-six percent of those surveyed believe that a good EPC

the value of a home, while just over half say the EPC label helps

That could become increasingly important. More than seven in 10
Belgians expect heatwaves in Belgium to become more frequent
and more intense. And three-quarters expect protection against
heat to become just as important as energy eﬃciency when
buying a home in the future. Heat resilience could therefore become
another factor in housing decisions, alongside EPC, location, size

and the overall condition of the property.
There are already signs that attitudes are shifting. Almost half of
Belgians say recent heatwaves have changed their views on the
investments needed in their home, with renovation intentions being
higher among homeowners who experienced longer periods of
heat discomfort.
Summer comfort can outweigh EPC
considerations
Making homes more resilient to heat does not necessarily mean
installing air conditioning everywhere. Belgians primarily favour

measures that prevent heat from entering the home. Nearly 80%
consider external shading, screens or shutters among the most
important measures for keeping a home cool, followed by
insulation adapted to hot weather and active cooling.
Still, six in 10 Belgians see a cooling system as more of a necessity
than a luxury, while a similar share believe that the government
should subsidise their installation. Interestingly, the impact on EPC
appears to carry relatively little weight when households consider a

cooling system. Although most Belgians associate a good EPC
rating with a higher property value, only 13% rank the impact on
the EPC rating among the most important criteria when choosing a
cooling system.
Moreover, one in three Belgians say they would not mind if
installing a cooling system worsened their EPC rating. This suggests
that when households face a trade-oﬀ between energy

performance and summer comfort, EPC is not always the deciding

High costs and an information gap
complicate the dual renovation challenge
Belgium's housing stock thus faces a dual renovation challenge.
Homes need to become more energy eﬃcient, while also becoming
better equipped to cope with hotter summers. But for many
households, the barriers remain substantial. Two in three Belgians

rank high investment costs among the three biggest obstacles to
making housing more energy eﬃcient, while half point to a lack of
own funds. Uncertainty about subsidies and government support,
future regulation and payback periods adds further complexity to
renovation decisions.
The challenge is not only ﬁnancial. There is also a clear information
gap. Almost two in three Belgians ﬁnd it diﬃcult to estimate
renovation costs upfront, while only one in three feel suﬃciently

informed about how to make their home more energy eﬃcient.
Total renovation costs are particularly diﬃcult to assess. Seven in
10 Belgians rank them among the three aspects most often
underestimated when carrying out energy renovations. Finding
suitable contractors, navigating the complexity of the renovation
process and estimating how long the works will take are also
frequently underestimated.
As renovations increasingly need to improve summer comfort as

well as energy performance, households need clear information on
which measures work, what they cost and how they aﬀect comfort
throughout the year. Preparing Belgium’s housing stock for the
future therefore means protecting homes not only against cold
winters, but also against warmer summers.

SpaceX 為全球 太空產業龍頭 ：SpaceX 業務主要分為 Connectivity 、AI

訊服務給大規模用戶的供應商。  
圖表一：衛星產業市場規模

資料來源： SIA ，中信投顧整理  
Falcon 9 開啟火箭技術革命 ： 傳統 火箭多為一次性 設計 ，主要結構於
發 射後 拋棄， 使每 次發射 任務 均須重 新製 造與整 備， 導致 火 箭發 射成
本高昂。而 SpaceX 的獵鷹 9 號(Falcon 9 )於 2015 年首次完成一級火箭
回收，並於 2017 年實現回收助推器再次飛行，突破火箭一次性使用限
制，降低火箭發射成本， 使衛星寬頻通訊商業化可行性提高。 Falcon 9
每次發射衛星 報價 約在 3,200 美元 /公斤 ，SpaceX 自用成本更低 ，而最
新 的傳 統火 箭(Ariane 6) 發射 報價 則 約在 6,000 美元 /公斤 ， 相比 之 下
SpaceX 在火箭發射成本上有顯著優勢。 而 下 一 代 火 箭星艦 (Starship)
推測每公斤成本約在 600 美元 /公斤， 且目標 為全部回收並 將發射成本
進一步降低至 200 美元 /公斤 ，有望進一步帶動衛星發射數量 。

## Document 419: “It’s the economy, stupid.” K字化が揺らす米国中間選挙

Source PDF: all report pdf/“It’s the economy, stupid.” K字化が揺らす米国中間選挙.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年 10 月 22 日 全9 頁

“It’s the economy, stupid.”  
K 字化が揺らす米国中間選挙 
中間選挙では、上下院で民主党が僅差で多数派になると予想

経済調査部 主任研究員 矢作 大祐

[要約] 
 2026 年11 月3 日の米中間選挙では、景気指標の底堅さと有権者の生活実感との乖離が
選挙結果を左右しうる。1992 年大統領選挙では、“It’s the economy, stupid.”を掲げ
た民主党のクリントン氏がブッシュ（父）氏に勝利した。米国の選挙では、有権者の
景気・物価に対する評価が選挙結果を予想する上でも重要になるといえよう。 
 足元の米国経済は実質GDP 成長率が前年比＋2％強と底堅く推移する一方、成長を牽引
するのは AI 関連投資や高所得層の消費に偏る。低中所得層では実質賃金の低下、貯蓄
余力の縮小、クレジットカードローン延滞率の上昇が重なり、景気拡大の恩恵を感じ
にくい「K 字経済（良し悪しが二分化する状態）」が鮮明になっている。 
 こうした低中所得層を中心とした生活苦は、トランプ大統領の支持率や消費者マイン
ドを押し下げ、与党共和党への逆風となっている。中間選挙のメインシナリオは、民
主党が上下院で僅差の多数派を獲得するというものだ。下院では民主党による多数派
奪還の公算が大きく、上院でも激戦州の結果次第で民主党が優位に立つとみる。ただ
し、民主党内の急進左派候補が無党派層を取り込めないリスクもあり、圧勝は見込み
にくい。 
 注目点は議席数だけでなく、民主党の「勝ち方」と共和党の「負け方」となる。各州
の結果は、2028 年大統領選挙を見据えた両党の主導権争いや、AI 規制・課税を含む経
済政策にも影響を及ぼしう る。そして、今回の中間選挙で民主党が躍進すれば、実質
GDP 成長率などのヘッドラインの数字だけでは捉えられない、米国社会の不満と経済
の脆弱性が浮き彫りになるだろう。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

経済情勢が左右する米中間選挙の結果 
2026 年 11 月 3 日に実施される米国中間選挙が近づいている。市場参加者の関心は、選挙後
の議会構成やトランプ政権の政策運営に集まっている。しかし、中間選挙を考える上で重要な
のは、足元の米国経済の実態を把握することだ。1992 年の大統領選挙で、民主党のビル・クリ
ントン氏は“It’s the economy, stupid.”（ 「重要なのは経済だ、愚か者」 ）という有名なスロ
ーガンを掲げ、当時の大統領であったブッシュ（父）氏に勝利した。景気や物価に対する有権
者の評価が選挙結果を左右するということは、米国における選挙の基本的な特徴といえる。 
もっとも、現在の米国経済は「好調」か「不調」か、という単純な二択では捉えにくい。経
済全体は底堅い一方、その内側では、成長の恩恵を受ける分野・主体と、恩恵を実感しにくい
分野・主体の差が広がっている。本稿では、こうした米国経済の K 字化（良し悪しが二分化す
る状態）の進展がトランプ大統領の支持率と中間選挙にどのような影響を及ぼしているのかを
整理し、中間選挙の注目点や見通しを提示する。

ヘッドラインは堅調、実態は K 字経済 
米国経済全体を俯瞰すると、一見して大きな問題は見当たらない。2026年10 月のIMF「世界
経済見通し」でも、米国は先進国の中で高い成長率を維持している国の一つと評価されている。
2026 年の実質 GDP 成長率は 4-6 月期までは前年比＋2％強と、おおむね潜在成長率並みのペー
スで推移している。このため、米国経済は堅調というのが一般的な見方だ。しかし、実質 GDP
の内訳を見ると様相は大きく異なる。米国経済の二枚看板である設備投資と個人消費を比較す
ると、設備投資が堅調である一方、個人消費はやや冴えない（図表1 左図） 。経済全体として成
長が続いていても、その構成項目は同じ方向を向いていない。好調な設備投資と軟調な個人消
費が併存する、いわゆるK 字経済の様相を呈している。 
また、K 字化は多層化している。設備投資に関してもAI 投資が積極化し、情報通信機器やソ
フトウェアなどのIT 関連投資は大幅なプラスとなっている一方、それ以外の投資はマイナス圏
に沈んでいる（図表 1 右図）。米国では、こうした状況を“Non-AI Recession”（「AI 以外の景
気後退」）と呼ぶ向きもある。AI 関連分野が投資全体を押し上げる裏側で、その他の産業には
停滞感が残っている。 
K 字化は企業部門だけの現象ではなく、個人消費も同様だ。実質小売販売額を所得階層別に
見ると、高所得層が相対的に高い伸びを維持している一方、低中所得層の伸びは弱い（図表 2
左図）。高所得層の消費が強い第一の理由 として、株高による資産効果 が挙げられる 。S&P500
が最高値を更新する中、株式や投資信託などのリスク資産を多く保有する家計ほど、資産価値
の上昇を通じて消費余力が高まりやすい。株・投信の保有残高に関する高所得層（上位20％）
対低中所得層（それ以外）の倍率は 7 倍程度かつ、上昇基調にあり、株高の恩恵は高所得層に
集中している（図表 2 右図）。また、第二の理由として、2025 年 7 月に成立した「トランプ減

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

税 2.0」もK 字化を助長していると考えられる1。いわゆるトランプ減税2.0 による所得階層別
の家計所得への影響に関しては、高所得層の改善幅が大きい一方、低中所得層への恩恵は相対
的に小さい。株高と減税が同時に高所得層の消費余力を支えることで、家計における K 字化が
さらに進みやすい構造となっている。

4 / 9

低中所得層は生活苦に直面し、与党共和党は逆風にさらされる 
株高や減税の恩恵が限られていても、賃金が十分に増えれば家計の不満は和らぐ。しかし、
足元では賃金の伸びが鈍化している 。物価上昇に加え、名目賃金の伸び自体も減速しており
（図表 3 左図） 、実質賃金は前年比でマイナスとなっている。労働所得への依存度が高い低中所
得層にとって、暮らし向きは改善しにくい。家計はこれまで、貯蓄に回す資金を抑えることで
消費を維持してきた。しかし、実質可処分所得が伸び悩む中で貯蓄率はすでに低水準にあり、
さらに貯蓄を削る余地は限られる。低中所得層のように多額の金融資産を保有していない家計
ほど、賃金の伸び悩みがそのまま消費余力の低下につながりやすい（図表3 右図）。 
図表 3 名目賃金と実質賃金、貯蓄率とクレジットカードローン延滞率

（出所）BLS、BEA、NY 連銀、Haver Analytics より大和総研作成

加えて、クレジットカードローンの延滞率はリーマン・ショック直後の水準に近づいている。
2022 年から 2023 年に貯蓄率が大幅に低下したこともあったが、当時はコロナ禍における超過
貯蓄があり、クレジットカードローン延滞率は低水準だった。貯蓄率が低下し、借り入れ状況
も悪化している中で、家計部門の消費余力は黄色信号がともっているといえよう。仮に株価が
調整し、高所得層の資産効果まで弱まれば、消費全体の下振れリスクは大きくなりかねない。 
こうした生活苦は政治にも影響を及ぼしている。トランプ大統領を支持するMAGA 派には、労
働所得に依存する低中所得層も多く含まれる。トランプ政権の下でも生活が改善しないと感じ
る有権者の不満は、政権への評価を押し下げている。例えば、トランプ大統領の支持率から不
支持率を差し引いたネット支持率は低下しており、連動する消費者センチメントも悪化してい
る（図表 4 左図）。分野別の支持動向をみても、生活コストと経済に対する評価が低い（図表 4
右図） 。つまり、現在の米国は経済全体が一定の成長を維持していても、その成長の恩恵がAI
関連企業、高所得層、金融資産保有者に偏っているため、低中所得層は景気の良さを実感しに

くいということだ。しかも、低中所得層は有権者の厚い層を占めており、彼らの暮らし向きの
悪化は、米国全体の景況感や政権に対する評価の悪化につながりやすいと考えられる。 
図表 4 消費者マインドとトランプ大統領の支持動向、分野別トランプ大統領の支持動向

（出所）Silver Bulletin、ロイター/ミシガン大より大和総研作成

メインシナリオは、上院・下院ともに民主党が僅差で多数派 
こうした経済環境や世論動向を踏まえると、今回の中間選挙は共和党にとって逆風となる公
算が大きい。現時点におけるメインシナリオは、上下院ともに民主党が多数派を獲得するケー
スとなる。中間選挙の基本的な仕組みを確認すると、上院では100 議席のうち35 議席、下院で
は全435 議席が改選される。上院は非改選議席を含めて51 議席、下院は218 議席を確保した政
党が多数派となる（図表5） 。 
特に下院については民主党優勢との見方が強い。下院は全議席が 2 年ごとに改選されるため、
政権や景気に対する有権者の評価が比較的反映されやすい。過去の中間選挙でも、下院におけ
る大統領所属政党の議席増減は、大統領支持率との連動性が高い。足元のトランプ大統領の支
持率は低水準かつ低下傾向にあり、下院共和党候補にとって厳しい環境といえる。現在の世論
調査を踏まえれば、民主党がすでに 220 議席程度を固めており、多数派を共和党から奪還する
公算が大きい。もっとも、民主党が下院で圧倒的な多数を得るとまではみていない。激戦区の
半分を民主党が制したと仮定すれば、民主党の議席は228 議席程度となる。多数派に必要な218
議席は上回るものの、共和党に対するリードは大きくない。下院のメインシナリオは「民主党
への交代」だが、その中身は圧勝ではなく、比較的僅差の多数派と捉えるべきだろう。 
より注目されるのは上院だ。上院議員の任期は 6 年で、一度の選挙で改選されるのは全体の
約 3 分の 1 に限られる。このため、上院選挙は大統領支持率との相関が下院ほど強くなく、候
補者個人の知名度や州固有の政治事情に左右されやすい。現時点の票読みでは、民主党が48 議

席、共和党が47 議席を確保する見込みで、残る5 議席が激戦区となっている。議席構成だけを
見れば極めて僅差であり、少数の激戦州の結果が多数派を決める。 
図表 5 上下院の議席構成・予想値

（注）2026 年10 月2 日時点。上院民主党には、無所属民主党会派も含む。上院に関しては、民主党は改選13 議
席、共和党は改選 22 議席。下院の現状における「激戦」は欠員を示す。 「有利」は“Likely”と“Leans”の合
計。 
（出所）270toWin より大和総研作成

それにもかかわらず、上院でも民主党が僅差で多数派を握るとみる理由として、共和党が今
回の選挙をトランプ大統領と強く結びつけてきたことが挙げられる2。9 月の共和党大会で、ト
ランプ大統領は、自身が出馬しているかのように共和党候補へ投票してほしいと訴えた。また、
激戦州の共和党候補にはMAGA 派や保守系の親トランプ派も見られ、有権者は候補者とトランプ
大統領のイメージを重ねやすい。この戦略は、トランプ大統領の支持率が高ければ共和党候補
への追い風となる一方、支持率が低迷する局面では逆風を上院選挙にも波及させうる。激戦州
の世論調査でも、MAGA 派候補は総じて苦戦している（図表6）。共和党がジョージア州やノース
カロライナ州よりも、立て直しの余地があるとみられるカンザス州やアイオワ州に選挙資金を
振り向け始めたとされ、激戦州とされる選挙区の中でも実際には民主党の勝利が近づいている
州もあると考えられる。 
ただし、上院で民主党が圧勝すると考えるのも早計だ。民主党側のリスクは、急進左派候補
が無党派層や「共和党も民主党も支持したくない」と考えるダブルヘイター層の票を十分に取
り込めない可能性といえる。民主党のコア支持層には歓迎される政策であっても、中道的な有
権者には投票をためらわせる場合がある。こうした取りこぼしを考慮すれば、上院のメインシ
ナリオは「民主党圧勝」ではなく、 「民主党が僅差で多数派を確保する」というものになる。

図表 6 上院選挙の注目州動向

（注）10 月 5 日時点の直近世論調査 3 つ（選挙区によって異なる）の平均値。共和党候補者の濃い赤色は MAGA
派、薄いピンクは保守・親トランプ派、白色は中道右派。民主党候補者の濃い青は急進左派、薄い青はリベラ
ル派、白色は中道左派。民主党優勢度は濃い青は民主党が明確に優勢、薄い青はやや優勢、濃いオレンジは共
和党が明確に優勢、薄いオレンジは共和党がやや優勢。 
（出所）Silver Bulletin より大和総研作成

中間選挙の注目点は民主党の「勝ち方」 、共和党の「負け方」 
上下院で民主党優勢との見方がコンセンサスになりつつあるが、最大の注目点は民主党の
「勝ち方」と共和党の「負け方」だ。仮に民主党が大勝した場合、2028 年の大統領選挙・議会
選挙に向けて共和党が勢力を立て直すハードルは高まる。たとえ共和党が大統領選挙で勝利し
たとしても、議会で民主党優位が続けば政権運営は容易ではない。一方、民主党にとっては今
回の中間選挙で大勝できれば、2028 年の議会選挙に対する不安を和らげることができるほか、
大統領選挙にも勝利した場合には、ホワイトハウスと上下両院を押さえる「オールブルー」に
近づく可能性が高まる。 
例えば、2028 年の大統領選挙・議会選挙も考慮した場合の上院の注目州については、ジョー
ジア州、テキサス州、オハイオ州、ミシガン州、メイン州、フロリダ州が挙げられる。共和党
にとって特に重要なのはテキサス州、オハイオ州、フロリダ州だ。テキサス州の上院選で共和
党が敗北すれば 1993 年の補欠選挙以来約 33 年ぶりとなり、トランプ大統領の求心力に疑問を
持つ共和党議員が増える可能性がある。また、オハイオ州はバンス副大統領の地盤であるため、
共和党候補を勝利に導けるかどうかは、バンス副大統領の党内求心力を測る材料となる。 
フロリダ州はトランプ大統領とルビオ国務長官の地盤であり、現状では共和党がおおむね優
勢とみられる。仮に共和党がオハイオ州で敗れる一方、フロリダ州を維持すれば、2028 年大統
領選挙を見据えた共和党内の主導権争いで、ルビオ国務長官の存在感が相対的に高まる可能性
